# [vllm] feat: Support LoRA for DeepSeek V4 & DeepSeek V4.1 & GLM5.3 Flash

[Upstream PR](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7483) · state: open · merged: False

## Original PR description

### What does this PR do?

Depends on:
- https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51368
- https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52552
- https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52626
- https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53361
- https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56327
- https://github.com/NVIDIA-NeMo/Megatron-Bridge/pull/6044
- https://github.com/NVIDIA-NeMo/Megatron-Bridge/pull/6199
- https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/pull/7054
- https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/pull/7521
- https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/pull/7522
- https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/pull/8018
- https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/pull/7763
- https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/pull/7762
- https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/pull/7500
- https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/pull/7501
- https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/pull/7502
- https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/pull/7503
- https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/pull/7678

Adds DeepSeek V4, DeepSeek V4.1 Flash and GLM5.3 Flash LoRA support to verl's vLLM weight-sync path. Previously a merge=False LoRA base sync orphaned the `.base_layer.` suffix on wrapped modules and mishandled the mxfp4 quant path, so DSV4 LoRA diverged from the actor.

This reworks weight-name reconciliation to be runtime-probed (strict vs flat loader) instead of model-type-based, unwraps the LoRA-installed mxfp4 quant-method wrapper, and threads `peft_config` through the FP8/mxfp4 load path so the same reconciliation applies there.

Tested with Megatron backend, GPU E2E (DSV4 merge=F GRPO, 2-node): step1/step2 pearson 0.9970/0.9950, aligned to run15 merge=T 0.9952/0.9944.

Unit tests cover strict-vs-flat loader detection, per-expert routed leaves, and non-LoRA params on wrapped modules (e.g. DSV4 `gate.tid2eid` / `gate.e_score_correction_bias`).

### API and Usage Example

For GLM-5.3-Flash, prepare every Ray node with a Bridge build providing `GLM53FlashModel` and its paired Megatron-Core, then launch with `VERL_USE_UV=0`. The frozen `megatron` extra pins Bridge 0.6.2, which lacks this wrapper. DSW CPU import checks passed against both the documented upstream source pair (Bridge `58382ddf`, Core `902feb8e`) and the separate Bridge `d16c26d5` environment used for GLM training. [Per-node setup and import check](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/blob/cd9330c25a9408aeedc87a5abfb88562e9318a05/examples/tuning/lora/run_glm53_flash_megatron.sh#L12-L19)

No CLI/API change. Behavior is automatic: `resolve_weight_name` probes the inner model's `load_weights` at runtime and toggles `.base_layer.` accordingly. For DSV4 merge=False LoRA, no extra config is needed beyond enabling LoRA + `merge=False`.

```bash
# DSV4 merge=False LoRA (existing config; no new flags)
actor_rollout_ref.model.lora.enable=True
actor_rollout_ref.model.lora.merge=False
actor_rollout_ref.model.lora.target_modules='["linear_wkv","linear_wgate","linear_wq_b","linear_weights_proj","linear_kv_proj","linear_q_down_proj","linear_q_up_proj","linear_proj","linear_fc1","linear_fc2","router"]'
```

### Design & Code Changes

**Runtime-probed `.base_layer.` reconciliation** (`verl/utils/vllm/utils.py`)
- Adds `_inner_load_weights_is_strict`: probes the inner transformer's `load_weights` bytecode (`co_names`) to decide strict (keeps `.base_layer.`, DeepseekV2/DSV4) vs flat (strips it, Llama/Qwen3.5). Cached per class.
- `resolve_weight_name` toggles one `.base_layer.` segment loader-style-aware, re-adds the suffix for non-LoRA params on a wrapped module, and reconciles per-expert routed leaves (strict keep / flat strip, gated by `_HAS_LORA_BASE_LAYER_PREFIX`).

**mxfp4 LoRA unwrap** (`verl/utils/vllm/vllm_fp4_utils.py`)
- Adds `_resolve_mxfp4_quant_method`: unwraps the `FusedMoEModularMethod` wrapper that merge=False LoRA installs over `Mxfp4MoEMethod` (stored as `old_quant_method`) so staging, layout derivation, and `process_weights_after_loading` repack reach the legacy method — the modular wrapper inherits a no-op post-load and would otherwise skip the repack.

**FP8/mxfp4 name reconciliation** (`verl/utils/vllm/vllm_quant_utils.py`, `verl/workers/rollout/vllm_rollout/utils.py`)
- `load_quanted_weights` gains a `peft_config` parameter; when set (LoRA base sync), names run through `resolve_weight_name` against live params/buffers so `.base_layer.` is reconciled on the FP8/mxfp4 path too (e.g. DSV4 `compressor.fused_wkv_wgate`). Callers thread `peft_config` for both base and drafter loads.

**LoRA MoE prefix** (`verl/utils/vllm/patch.py`)
- Removes the eager `lora_base_layer_prefix` clearing block; the prefix is now reconciled through `resolve_weight_name` instead.

**Tests** (`tests/utils/test_vllm_weight_name_normalization_on_cpu.py`)
- Expanded coverage for strict-vs-flat loader detection, per-expert routed leaves, and non-LoRA params on wrapped modules.

### Review follow-up validation

<summary>examples/tuning/lora/run_deepseek_v4_flash_megatron.sh (1)</summary><blockquote>

`28-28`: **📐 Maintainability & Code Quality** | **🔵 Trivial** | **💤 Low value**

**Guard `HDFS_ROOT` and `DATA_ROOT` against unset values.**

The script uses `set -euo pipefail`. Lines 28, 84, and 85 expand `$HDFS_ROOT` and `$DATA_ROOT` inside the default-value text. If the variable is unset and no override is set, the script aborts with an "unbound variable" error. The message does not tell the user which variable to set.

Use `${HDFS_ROOT:?...}` and `${DATA_ROOT:?...}`, or set a default. This gives a clear error message.

<details>
<summary>Proposed fix</summary>

```diff
-MODEL_PATH=${MODEL_PATH:-$HDFS_ROOT/model/DeepSeek-V4-Flash}
+MODEL_PATH=${MODEL_PATH:-${HDFS_ROOT:?set HDFS_ROOT or MODEL_PATH}/model/DeepSeek-V4-Flash}
```
</details>

> 
> **Pass the merged `hf_overrides` to vLLM.**
> 
> If `engine_kwargs.vllm.hf_overrides` is set and rollout FP8 quantization is enabled, `_apply_quantization()` adds `quantization_config` to this copy. In `launch_server()`, the later `**engine_kwargs` entry replaces that copy with the original value. vLLM therefore does not receive the generated FP8 configuration or ignored-layer list. Remove `hf_overrides` from `engine_kwargs` before the spread, or assign the merged value after the spread. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/vllm-project/vllm/v0.29.0/vllm/model_executor/model_loader/weight_utils.py))

<details>
<summary>♻️ Duplicate comments: 1</summary><blockquote>

<details>
<summary>verl/utils/vllm/vllm_fp8_utils.py: 1</summary><blockquote>

`339-352`: **🎯 Functional Correctness** | **🟠 Major** | **⚡ Quick win**

**Resolve the LoRA wrapper before you select the processor. The `else` unwrap does not run for the real wrapper.**

- Lines 337-338 read `process_weights_after_loading` from `layer.quant_method`. On a merge=False layer, that object is `FusedMoEModularMethod`.
- `vllm_fp4_utils.py` Lines 592-595 state that this wrapper inherits a no-op `process_weights_after_loading` from the vLLM base class. For the wrapper, `process` is therefore not `None`.
- The `if` branch calls the no-op. `_resolve_fp8_moe_quant_method` at Line 349 never runs.
- The leftover loop at Lines 358-359 then folds the checkpoint-layout staging data into the live parameters without the kernel repack. LoRA FP8 MoE rollouts then run on weights in the wrong layout.
- The CPU test does not catch this defect. `_LoraModularMethod` in `tests/utils/test_vllm_fp8_restage_same_shape_on_cpu.py` has no hook, but the real wrapper has one. Give the stub a no-op `process_weights_after_loading` so that the stub matches vLLM.

The previous commits fixed the `NameError`. They did not fix the branch selection.

<details>
<summary>Proposed fix</summary>

```diff
-        quant_method = getattr(layer, "quant_method", None)
-        process = getattr(quant_method, "process_weights_after_loading", None)
         # Patch replace_parameter so the repack folds into the live storage
         # the CUDA graph captured, not a fresh Parameter (which leaves the
         # captured storage zero). Both branches below repack under it.
         from vllm.model_executor.layers.quantization import fp8 as _vllm_fp8
 
-        if process is not None:
-            with patch.object(_vllm_fp8, "replace_parameter", replace_parameter_preserve_subclass):
-                process(layer)
-        else:
-            # merge=False no-op wrapper: unwrap to Fp8MoEMethod and repack.
-            resolved = _resolve_fp8_moe_quant_method(layer)
-            if resolved is not None:
-                with patch.object(_vllm_fp8, "replace_parameter", replace_parameter_preserve_subclass):
-                    resolved.process_weights_after_loading(layer)
+        # merge=False wraps Fp8MoEMethod in a wrapper whose post-load hook is a no-op: unwrap first.
+        quant_method = _resolve_fp8_moe_quant_method(layer) or getattr(layer, "quant_method", None)
+        process = getattr(quant_method, "process_weights_after_loading", None)
+        if process is not None:
+            with patch.object(_vllm_fp8, "replace_parameter", replace_parameter_preserve_subclass):
+                process(layer)
```
</details>

<details>
<summary>verl/utils/qat/compressed_tensors.py: 1</summary><blockquote>

`528-530`: **🎯 Functional Correctness** | **🔵 Trivial** | **💤 Low value**

⚠️ **Unverified finding**
Verification ran but could not confirm this finding. It is shown for review, not as a verified issue.

**Handle invalid or non-mapping `hf_overrides` explicitly.**

A string value is parsed with `json.loads`. A malformed string raises a bare `JSONDecodeError` with no context. A valid JSON value that is not an object, such as `[]` or `"x"`, passes the `or {}` fallback or reaches `"quantization_config" not in hf_overrides` and gives a wrong result or `TypeError`. An empty string is handled, because `or {}` turns it into `{}`.

Add a clear error for these cases. This path is a misconfiguration, so the impact is low.

<details>
<summary>Proposed fix</summary>

```diff
     if isinstance(hf_overrides, str):
-        hf_overrides = json.loads(hf_overrides)
+        try:
+            hf_overrides = json.loads(hf_overrides)
+        except json.JSONDecodeError as e:
+            raise ValueError(f"rollout.engine_kwargs.vllm.hf_overrides is not valid JSON: {e}") from e
+    if not isinstance(hf_overrides, Mapping):
+        raise TypeError(f"hf_overrides must be a mapping, got {type(hf_overrides).__name__}")
```
</details>

</blockquote></details>

</blockquote></details>

> **🎯 Functional Correctness** | **🟠 Major** | **⚡ Quick win**
> 
> **Preserve the strict loader’s attribute prefix.**
> 
> When `.model.load_weights` is strict, this branch returns `""` instead of `"model."`. `_name_reaches_strict_loader` then treats sibling names such as `lm_head.*` as strict-loader names. For a LoRA-wrapped sibling, `resolve_weight_name` can retain `.base_layer.` even though the sibling is loaded through `AutoWeightsLoader`; its `BaseLayerWithLoRA.load_weights` forwards the remaining name to the base layer. Include the child attribute in the detected prefix, and add a sibling-name case to the probe tests. vLLM 0.29.0 has this `.model` and `.lm_head` layout. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/vllm-project/vllm/v0.29.0/vllm/model_executor/models/deepseek_v2.py))
>

> [!CAUTION]
> Some comments are outside the diff and can’t be posted inline due to GitHub limitations.
> 
> **⚠️ Outside diff range comments (1)**
> 
> <details>
> <summary><strong><picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/major-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Major</strong> · Do not emit <code>vision_token_types</code> without image tensors. · <code>multiturn_sft_dataset.py:384-385</code></summary><blockquote>
> <dl>
> <dd>
> 
> `verl/utils/dataset/multiturn_sft_dataset.py:384-385`
> **🩺 Stability & Availability** | **🟠 Major** | **⚡ Quick win**
> 
> **Do not emit `vision_token_types` without image tensors.**
> 
> The DeepSeek V4.1 processor returns `vision_token_types` for text-only input, but omits `pixel_values` and `image_grid_hws`. The V4.1 model-forward path still calls `build_deepseek_v41_image_inputs()`. That helper indexes both missing keys and can raise `KeyError`.
> 
> Add the row only when both image tensors are present:
> 
> <details>
> <summary>Proposed fix</summary>
> <dl>
> <dd>
> 
> ```diff
> -        if vision_token_types is not None:
> +        if vision_token_types is not None and all(
> +            key in multi_modal_inputs for key in ("pixel_values", "image_grid_hws")
> +        ):
>              multi_modal_inputs["vision_token_types"] = vision_token_types
> ```
> 
> For mixed-turn samples, image turns provide both keys, so the row remains available for image processing. For text-only samples, the multimodal dictionary remains empty. This also prevents the truncation guard from treating an overlength text-only sample as multimodal. The truncation issue is separate from the image-input `KeyError`.
> 
> </dd>
> </dl>
> </details>
> 
> <!-- ai-agent-prompt:start -->
> 
> <details>
> <summary><picture><source media="(prefers-color-scheme: dark)" srcset="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/bot-dark.svg"><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/bot.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Prompt for AI Agents</summary>

**Update the fake engine interface.**

When vLLM is installed, the test invokes `server.generate(...)`, which calls `_generate_admitted()` and then `await self.engine.add_request(...)`. The fake engine defines only `generate`, so each case raises `AttributeError` before the prompt-alignment assertions run. Add an async `add_request` fake that returns a compatible result queue.

> [!CAUTION]
> Some comments are outside the diff and can’t be posted inline due to GitHub limitations.
> 
> **⚠️ Outside diff range comments (1)**
> 
> <details>
> <summary><strong><picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/amber-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Minor</strong> · Reject HybridModel before enabling fused forwarding. · <code>model_forward_fused.py:184-186</code></summary><blockquote>
> <dl>
> <dd>
> 
> `verl/models/mcore/model_forward_fused.py:184-186`
> **<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/amber-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Minor** · **Stability & Availability** · **Quick win**
> 
> **Reject HybridModel before enabling fused forwarding.**
> 
> `HybridModel.forward` in the pinned Megatron Core `core_v0.19.2` has no output-processor hook and no `_preprocess` method. `_get_patching_model` still returns the wrapper, so legacy mode installs `_fused_GPTModel_forward`, which calls `model._preprocess` and can fail immediately.
> 
> Returning `None` from `_get_patching_model` is not sufficient. `_maybe_enable_fused_kernels` must propagate the unsupported result and disable fused forwarding; otherwise `_get_fused_forward_mode` defaults to legacy and the fused engine still runs.
> 
> <details>
> <summary>Suggested fix</summary>
> <dl>
> <dd>
> 
> ```diff
>      inner = getattr(model, "language_model", model)
>      if HybridModel is not None and isinstance(inner, HybridModel):
> -        # Hybrid VLMs expose the output_processor hook on the wrapper itself.
> -        return model
> +        if _supports_output_processor_hook(model) or _supports_output_processor_hook(inner):
> +            return model
> +        print(f"Model {model.__class__.__name__} has no output_processor hook; fused forward is not supported")
> +        return None
> ```

> `verl/models/mcore/model_forward_fused.py:398`
> **<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/major-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Major** · **Stability & Availability** · **Quick win**
> 
> **Unpack the `(output, loss_mask)` tuple before the fused path reads `log_probs`.**
> 
> When the full-stream VLM wrapper runs in language-model-hook mode and returns a tuple, `output_orig` stays a tuple. The next read of `output_orig.log_probs` (and `output_orig.entropy` when entropy is enabled) then raises `AttributeError`. When `post_process` is false, the tuple is returned to the caller unchanged. The standard forward path already unpacks this tuple. The fused path does not.
> 
> <details>
> <summary>Suggested fix</summary>
> <dl>
> <dd>
> 
> ```diff
>                  with language_model_forward_kwargs(model, **hook_kwargs, compute_mtp_loss=False):
>                      output_orig = model(**forward_kwargs)
> +                if vision_model and takes_full_packed_thd_vlm_inputs(model) and isinstance(output_orig, tuple):
> +                    # The full-stream model returns its CP-sliced loss mask alongside the output.
> +                    output_orig = output_orig[0]
> ```
> 
> </dd>
> </dl>
> </details>
> 
> The full-stream wrapper test in `tests/models/test_model_forward_fused.py` should also return a tuple. Its current wrapper returns a single output object, so it does not cover this path.
>

> **<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/major-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Major** · **Data Integrity & Integration** · **Heavy lift**
> 
> **Add `-1` rows for samples that lack `vision_token_types`.**
> 
> `extract_multi_modal_inputs` emits one `vision_token_types` row per sample that has the key. A text-only sample has no row, so the row count can fall below the batch size. `build_deepseek_v41_image_inputs` then reads row `batch_index` as batch sample `batch_index`, so image spans after a text-only sample can be assigned to the wrong sample. Padding the image rows does not add the missing rows. Emit an all-`-1` placeholder row for each sample that lacks the key, in batch order.
> 
> <details>
> <summary>Suggested fix</summary>
> <dl>
> <dd>
> 
> ```diff
>      for key, values in multi_modal_inputs_collected.items():
>          if has_image_bound:  # minicpm-o logic
>              multi_modal_inputs[key] = values
> +        elif key == "vision_token_types":
> +            # Text-only samples omit this key. Emit an all -1 row for each one so rows stay aligned with batch samples.
> +            rows = []
> +            for inputs in selected_batch_data:
> +                inputs = inputs.data if isinstance(inputs, NonTensorData) else inputs
> +                value = None if inputs is None else inputs.get(key)
> +                rows.append(value if value is not None else torch.full((1, 0), -1, dtype=torch.int64))
> +            multi_modal_inputs[key] = _pad_last_dim_and_cat(rows, key)
>          elif key in _VARLEN_MULTI_MODAL_KEYS:
> ```
> 
> </dd>

> 
> `verl/utils/vllm/vllm_quant_utils.py:355-360`
> **<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/amber-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Minor** · **Functional Correctness** · **Quick win**
> 
> **Restrict scale dropping to unquantized live dtypes.**
> 
> `peft_config` can reach `load_quanted_weights` for compressed-tensors models, not only FP8 models. The repository’s mxfp4/NVFP4 layouts use `torch.uint8` packed live weights. These weights bypass the earlier BF16 FP8-pairing branch, so the current condition skips their `weight_scale` or `weight_scale_inv`. The weight is then loaded without replacing the live scale, which can leave the rollout model out of sync.
> 
> Limit the scale drop to live dtypes known to represent unquantized parameters.
> 
> <details>
> <summary>Proposed fix</summary>
> <dl>
> <dd>
> 
> ```diff
>                  if (
>                      leaf in {"weight_scale", "weight_scale_inv"}
> -                    and live_dtype is not None
> -                    and live_dtype not in fp8_dtypes
> +                    and live_dtype in {torch.bfloat16, torch.float16, torch.float32}
>                  ):
>                      continue
> ```
> 
> </dd>
> </dl>
> </details>
>

>

> **Pad missing rows to the full input length.**

> 
> Pass the effective input lengths to this extraction path and pad the merged rows to the required input width.
>

## Follow-up Comments

<details>
<summary><sub><picture><source media="(prefers-color-scheme: dark)" srcset="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-finding-badges-v1/contrast-v2/outside-diff-dark.svg" /><source media="(prefers-color-scheme: light)" srcset="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-finding-badges-v1/outside-diff.svg" /><img width="120" height="16" alt="Outside diff" src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-finding-badges-v1/outside-diff.svg" /></picture></sub> Reject null DeepSeek-V4 quantization overrides.</summary><blockquote>
<dl>
<dd>

`817-821`: **<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/major-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Major** · **Data Integrity & Integration** · **Quick win**

**Reject null DeepSeek-V4 quantization overrides.**

A native DeepSeek-V4 checkpoint stores `quantization_config` at the top level. It has no `text_config.quantization_config`. Therefore, `hf_overrides.quantization_config=null` leaves vLLM unquantized and reaches `return None` here.

The trainer then exports the unchanged Megatron-Bridge weights because no rollout quantizer is installed. DeepSeek-V4's native mixed FP8/FP4 layout is not converted to BF16, so the rollout can fail to load or consume synchronized weights.

Extend the existing guard to reject a null effective configuration:

<details>
<summary>Suggested fix</summary>
<dl>
<dd>

```diff
         if (
             has_hf_override
             and model_types & _DEEPSEEK_V4_MODEL_TYPES
-            and quantization_config is not None
-            and str(quantization_config.get("quant_method", "")).lower() == "compressed-tensors"
+            and (
+                quantization_config is None
+                or str(quantization_config.get("quant_method", "")).lower() == "compressed-tensors"
+            )
         ):
```

</dd>
</dl>
</details>

[Open the source location](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/blob/7961dce052f6e6b80527f14ab0895f996e12a8f2/verl/utils/qat/compressed_tensors.py#L817-L821)

<!-- ai-agent-prompt:start -->

<summary>🤖 Prompt for AI Agents</summary>

<dd>

Update the DeepSeek-V4 override guard using has_hf_override and
_DEEPSEEK_V4_MODEL_TYPES so it rejects an effective quantization_config that is
null as well as one using compressed-tensors; preserve the existing behavior for
other configurations.
```

<!-- ai-agent-prompt:end -->

> **<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/major-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Major** · **Stability & Availability** · **Heavy lift**
> 
> **Gather BSHD routes across context-parallel ranks.**
> 
> When R2 recording uses BSHD with static context parallelism greater than one, each rank records only its local sequence shard. The BSHD guard rejects only dynamic CP. The existing sequence-parallel gather does not restore static context-parallel shards. The code then compares the local row count with the full input lengths, so it can raise `BSHD router replay recorded only ... rows`.
> 
> Gather and restore the context-parallel route shards before calculating `padded_seqlen` and trimming routes. The correction belongs in `verl/utils/megatron/router_replay_utils.py:342-367` (with any required collective import near line 33), not in the configuration dictionary at `verl/workers/engine/megatron/transformer_impl.py:356-360`.
>

> 
> **Use a Bridge build that provides `GLM53FlashModel`.**
> 
> The launcher uses the frozen `megatron` extra, but that extra pins Megatron-Bridge to upstream `0.6.2`. That version does not provide `megatron.bridge.models.glm5next.glm53flash_bridge.GLМ53FlashModel`. Consequently, `takes_full_packed_thd_vlm_inputs` catches the import error and returns `False`, so the GLM-5.3 path falls back to ordinary VLM input handling.
> 
> Update the dependency to the Bridge build that contains `GLM53FlashModel`, or add an explicit documented installation step for that build before launching GLM-5.3.
>

> **Register `glm_moe_dsa` as a multimodal continuous-token family.**
> 
> The GLM-5.3-Flash launcher sends image prompts through the vLLM agent-loop rollout. Its supported `Glm5NextProcessor` reaches `create_continuous_token_builder`, but `glm_moe_dsa` resolves to the text-only `GLMContinuousTokenBuilder`. Because `GLM5` has no VL mapping, construction raises `ValueError` before the rollout can process a prompt.
> 
> Register a compatible VL or unified family for GLM-5.3-Flash. The inspected source does not establish that `GLM46VContinuousTokenBuilder` is compatible, so do not add an alias without validating that contract. The source also does not prove that a new builder is mandatory.

## Summary by CodeRabbit

* **New Features**
  * Added LoRA training examples for DeepSeek-V4 Flash, DeepSeek-V4.1 Flash, and GLM-5.3-Flash.
  * Added DeepSeek-V4.1 text and vision-language support, including image handling and configurable reasoning-effort budgets.
  * Added GLM-5.3 vision-language support and expanded multimodal routing replay compatibility.
  * Expanded rollout quantization options and support for multimodal model weight updates.

* **Bug Fixes**
  * Improved quantized weight updates and refreshed derived weights after reloads.
  * Preserved vision-token data during multimodal padding and dataset processing.
  * Improved routing alignment, generation resumption after weight updates, and fused-forward compatibility.
  * Prevented checkpoint saves when saving is disabled and improved model configuration loading fallbacks.

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->
The PR adds DeepSeek V4.1 and GLM-5.3 multimodal support, extends Megatron router replay, updates vLLM weight loading, quantization, and synchronization, and adds three LoRA training launchers. It also changes model configuration and trainer behavior.

**Changes: Multimodal training and runtime updates**

|Layer / File(s)|Summary|
|:---|:---|
|**DeepSeek tokenization and processor wiring** <br> `verl/utils/tokenizer/deepseek.py`, `verl/utils/tokenizer/continuous_token_wiring.py`, `verl/utils/tokenizer/tokenizer.py`, `tests/utils/test_continuous_token_on_cpu.py`|DeepSeek builders add image-token expansion and V4.1 reasoning-effort budgets. Processor and family wiring recognizes DeepSeek V4/V4.1 VL and GLM5 Next.|
|**Multimodal data and model-forward paths** <br> `verl/utils/dataset/multiturn_sft_dataset.py`, `verl/trainer/ppo/v1/agent_loop_tq.py`, `verl/trainer/ppo/padding_utils.py`, `verl/utils/model.py`, `verl/models/mcore/*`, `verl/workers/rollout/utils.py`, `tests/models/*`, `tests/trainer/ppo/v1/*`|Dataset and agent-loop paths preserve vision-token metadata and expand DeepSeek V4.1 image inputs. Megatron forward paths add image inputs, full-stream THD processing, and additional fused-forward hook modes. GLM image-token deduplication is also updated.|

**Megatron router replay**

|Layer / File(s)|Summary|
|:---|:---|
|**Route recording, alignment, and replay** <br> `verl/utils/megatron/router_replay_utils.py`, `verl/workers/engine/megatron/transformer_impl.py`, `verl/trainer/ppo/v1/trainer_sync.py`, `tests/utils/megatron/*`, `tests/workers/engine/megatron/*`|Replay supports multimodal route alignment and masks, BSHD and THD layouts, hybrid-layer detection, and route mapping across module and MoE layers. Engine wiring passes multimodal inputs and layout settings to replay helpers.|
|**DeepSeek V4 modality-router replay** <br> `verl/utils/megatron/router_replay_patch.py`|Adds optional ModalityRouter support that records or replays expert choices and applies modality-specific bias and padding-row handling.|

**vLLM weight loading and runtime updates**

|Layer / File(s)|Summary|
|:---|:---|
|**Weight resolution and quantized loading** <br> `verl/utils/vllm/utils.py`, `verl/utils/vllm/patch.py`, `verl/utils/vllm/vllm_quant_utils.py`, `verl/utils/vllm/vllm_fp8_utils.py`, `verl/utils/vllm/vllm_fp4_utils.py`, `tests/utils/test_vllm_weight_name_normalization_on_cpu.py`, `tests/utils/test_vllm_fp8_restage_same_shape_on_cpu.py`|Weight resolution handles loader-specific LoRA and routed-expert names. Quantized loading adds FP8-to-BF16 normalization and processes wrapped FP8 and MXFP4 methods.|
|**Weight synchronization and rollout behavior** <br> `verl/workers/rollout/vllm_rollout/*`, `verl/checkpoint_engine/base.py`, `tests/workers/rollout/test_vllm_weight_update_utils_on_cpu.py`|Weight updates run adapter post-processing and refresh derived caches. Rollout handling preserves quantization overrides and checks routing-replay prompts.|

**Quantization and training runtime compatibility**

|Layer / File(s)|Summary|
|:---|:---|
|**Quantizer dispatch and model configuration** <br> `verl/utils/qat/compressed_tensors.py`, `verl/workers/config/model.py`, `verl/utils/model.py`, `verl/utils/megatron_utils.py`, `tests/utils/test_mxfp8_ignored_layers_on_cpu.py`, `tests/utils/test_rollout_quantization_overrides_on_cpu.py`, `tests/utils/test_vllm_kv_cache_scale_reset_on_cpu.py`|Quantizer dispatch honors vLLM quantization overrides and normalizes ignored-layer names. Model configuration applies custom chat templates to tokenizer and processor and falls back to vLLM config loading on `ValueError`. Megatron loading also checks CPU-resident parameter IDs and an alternate FSDP implementation.|
|**Trainer and checkpoint behavior** <br> `verl/trainer/sft_trainer.py`, `verl/experimental/agent_loop/single_turn_agent_loop.py`, `verl/checkpoint_engine/base.py`|SFT checkpoint saving requires a positive save frequency. Naive checkpoint updates resume generation replicas, and the single-turn agent loop passes routed-expert data through without truncation.|

**Megatron LoRA launchers**

|Layer / File(s)|Summary|
|:---|:---|
|**DeepSeek V4.1-Flash launcher** <br> `examples/tuning/lora/run_deepseek_v41_flash_megatron.sh`|Adds LoRA target scopes, GRPO and rollout settings, validation for tensor parallelism and batch divisibility, and dry-run support.|
|**DeepSeek V4 launcher** <br> `examples/tuning/lora/run_deepseek_v4_flash_megatron.sh`|Adds GRPO and Megatron training configuration, conditional context-parallel and FP8 settings, and uv or Python launch selection.|
|**GLM-5.3-Flash launcher** <br> `examples/tuning/lora/run_glm53_flash_megatron.sh`|Adds GRPO and LoRA settings, context-parallel configuration, conditional FP8 behavior, and uv or Python launch selection.|

<!-- architecture_review_label
### Security Architecture Review

**Security architecture risk:** **🔵 Low** · up to `df420`
-->

No introduced privilege escalation or unauthorized data-access path was established in the inspected flows. The changes affect live state, however, and external compatibility and failure-recovery behavior remain partly unverified.

**Retained concerns**
No architecture-level concerns identified.

<details>
<summary><strong>Security review details</strong></summary>
<dl>
<dd>

**Security Blast Radius**
- _inferred_ — A faulty trusted weight update can affect the main rollout model, a synchronized drafter, and multiple configured replicas. The inspected changes do not establish a new route from dataset content to weight-update authority; host, tenant, and environment isolation were not verified.

**Trust Boundaries and Controls**
- _observed_ — Request admission is closed before engine pause, abort errors propagate, and resume starts engine scheduling before reopening admission. Adapter tensors retained by the loader are copied away from reusable transfer buffers.

**Resilience and Maintainability Implications**
- _inferred_ — Failure containment depends on keeping generation unavailable after an unsuccessful refit. The inspected success-only resume ordering supports that containment, but it does not prove that retrying a partially applied reload is idempotent or restores all staged layouts and caches.

**Hardening Proposals**
- _proposed_ — Define an explicit failed-refit recovery contract: keep affected replicas unavailable until restart or a verified complete reload has restored weights, layouts, and caches, rather than assuming a repeated transfer is safe.

</dd>
</dl>
</details>
<!-- architecture_review_end -->

</dd>
</dl>
</details>

### Chat

There are 3 ways to chat with [CodeRabbit](https://coderabbit.ai?utm_source=oss&utm_medium=github&utm_campaign=verl-project/verl&utm_content=7483):

- Review comments: Directly reply to a review comment made by CodeRabbit. Example:
  - `I pushed a fix in commit <commit_id>, please review it.`
  - `Open a follow-up GitHub issue for this discussion.`
- Files and specific lines of code (under the "Files changed" tab): Tag `@coderabbitai` in a new review comment at the desired location with your query.
- PR comments: Tag `@coderabbitai` in a new PR comment to ask questions about the PR branch. For the best results, please provide a very specific query, as very limited context is provided in this mode. Examples:
  - `@coderabbitai gather interesting stats about this repository and render them as a table. Additionally, render a pie chart showing the language distribution in the codebase.`
  - `@coderabbitai read the files in the src/scheduler package and generate a class diagram using mermaid and a README in the markdown format.`

### CodeRabbit commands

> These commands are invoked using PR/Issue comments.

- `@coderabbitai pause` to pause the reviews on a PR.
- `@coderabbitai resume` to resume the paused reviews.
- `@coderabbitai review` to trigger an incremental review. This is useful when automatic reviews are disabled for the repository.
- `@coderabbitai full review` to do a full review from scratch and review all the files again.
- `@coderabbitai review --preview-config` to test the unmerged CodeRabbit configuration on a draft PR. The requester must have repository write access; preview results are non-authoritative.
- `@coderabbitai rate limit` to show your current review rate limit status.
- `@coderabbitai summary` to regenerate the summary of the PR.
- `@coderabbitai track` in a human-authored review thread to track the original finding until CodeRabbit verifies it is fixed.
- `@coderabbitai fixed` in a tracked review thread to verify the current code against the original finding.
- `@coderabbitai evaluate custom pre-merge check --instructions <custom-checks-instructions> --name <custom-checks-title> [--mode <error|warning>]` to validate the custom pre-merge checks instructions. Defaults to `error` when `--mode` is omitted.
- `@coderabbitai ignore pre-merge checks` to override pre-merge checks and get an approval on PR.
- `@coderabbitai run pre-merge checks` to run pre-merge checks on the pull request.
- `@coderabbitai generate sequence diagram` to generate a sequence diagram of the changes in this PR.
- `@coderabbitai emit path instructions` to emit generated path-specific review instructions for this repository.
- `@coderabbitai generate project vocabulary` to list vocabulary that is specific to this repository.
- `@coderabbitai resolve merge conflict` to automatically resolve merge conflicts.
- `@coderabbitai autofix` to automatically fix issues in the current review thread, or all unresolved review comments when used in the PR conversation.
- `@coderabbitai autopilot` to enable Autofix to fix CodeRabbit findings and required CI, and resolve merge conflicts.
- `@coderabbitai autopilot resume` to resume Autofix for this PR.
- `@coderabbitai autopilot stop` to stop Autofix for this PR.
- `@coderabbitai fix-ci` to automatically fix failing CI checks in a stacked pull request.
- `@coderabbitai fix-ci commit` to automatically fix failing CI checks by committing fixes to the current branch.
- `@coderabbitai resolve` to resolve all the CodeRabbit review comments.
- `@coderabbitai configuration` to show the current CodeRabbit configuration for the repository.
- `@coderabbitai generate configuration` to create a PR that adds the current resolved configuration as `.coderabbit.yaml` (or show it if already present).
- `@coderabbitai help` to get help.

### Other keywords and placeholders

- Add `@coderabbitai ignore` or `@coderabbit ignore` anywhere in the PR description to prevent this PR from being reviewed.
- Add `@coderabbitai summary` to generate the high-level summary at a specific location in the PR description.
- Add `@coderabbitai` anywhere in the PR title to generate the title automatically.

### CodeRabbit configuration file (`.coderabbit.yaml`)

- You can programmatically configure CodeRabbit by adding a `.coderabbit.yaml` file to the root of your repository.
- Please see the [configuration documentation](https://docs.coderabbit.ai/guides/configure-coderabbit) for more information.
- You can also validate your configuration using the [online YAML validator](https://docs.coderabbit.ai/configuration/yaml-validator).
- If your editor has YAML language server enabled, you can add the path at the top of this file to enable auto-completion and validation: `# yaml-language-server: $schema=https://coderabbit.ai/integrations/schema.v2.json`


### Status, support, documentation and community

- Visit our [status page](https://status.coderabbit.ai) to check the current availability of CodeRabbit.
- Create a ticket on our [support page](https://www.coderabbit.ai/contact-us/support) for assistance with any issues or questions.
- Visit our [documentation site](https://docs.coderabbit.ai) for detailed information on how to use CodeRabbit.
- Join our [Discord community](http://discord.gg/coderabbit) to connect with other users and get help from the community.
- Follow us on [X/Twitter](https://twitter.com/coderabbitai) for updates and announcements.

- HollowMan6 · comment · 2026-11-03T23:17:50Z · [source](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7483#issuecomment-6617959203)

Rechecked all inline findings and the actionable items embedded in earlier review summaries against the current source. The merge-risk text still refers to `caa67661`; the subsequent fixes are now pushed.

The functional fixes are in [14d666d1](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/commit/14d666d1b8a1aa467811aebca4a92bfbdb38a97c). The fused-forward test configuration and remaining launcher follow-ups are also pushed. Every new commit is GPG-signed and has only the author's Signed-off-by trailer.

Outside-diff and general-review findings have the following outcomes.

- [5489854872](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7483#pullrequestreview-5489854872) and [5525114601](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7483#pullrequestreview-5525114601): removed the redundant GLM DATA-array continuation in [ad086ea8](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/commit/ad086ea80e116cddf7e476839a3ba3267a091129). The DeepSeek V4 launcher retains its original root/path configuration; no new environment-variable validation was added. Both launcher syntax checks and a stubbed GLM argument check passed.
- [5504901796](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7483#pullrequestreview-5504901796): already addressed. The merged hf_overrides is written after the engine_kwargs expansion, so it is not replaced by the original input.
- [5576399141](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7483#pullrequestreview-5576399141) and [5577810069](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7483#pullrequestreview-5577810069): fixed the inherited no-op FP8 LoRA hook, shared hf_overrides validation, and exact strict-loader subtree scope. Tests cover old and delegating wrappers, live storage, mapped VLM prefixes, sibling lm_head weights, and genuinely strict roots.
- [5645089780](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7483#pullrequestreview-5645089780) and [5645305674](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7483#pullrequestreview-5645305674): already addressed. The multimodal SFT path rejects truncation that would break image alignment and emits vision_token_types only with the matching image tensors.
- [5657608990](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7483#pullrequestreview-5657608990), [5686797119](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7483#pullrequestreview-5686797119), and [5709398773](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7483#pullrequestreview-5709398773): the obsolete engine-stub test was removed. The replacement suite calls the real prompt-comparison helper. It distinguishes V4.1 compact expansion from other models and rejects mismatched already-expanded spans, including equal-total-length shifts and missing adjacent images.
- [5725286496](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7483#pullrequestreview-5725286496): already addressed. Unsupported HybridModel builds decline fused patching and the engine falls back to unfused forward. The backend-selection and wrapper-chain tests pass on the DSW Megatron installation.
- [5728820172](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7483#pullrequestreview-5728820172): no tuple-unpacking change is needed for the supported full-stream GLM53 wrapper. The fused call supplies labels but no loss_mask. The inspected Bridge wrapper returns the hook output directly in that case; its tuple return requires a non-None loss_mask and reordering. This conclusion is specific to that wrapper contract.
- [5729715221](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7483#pullrequestreview-5729715221): batch order is preserved with explicit text rows. Scale dropping is restricted to unquantized BF16/FP16/FP32 targets; the int8 scale regression passes.
- [5729850348](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7483#pullrequestreview-5729850348): fixed omitted modality rows using each sample's effective input length. A supplied but short row still raises instead of hiding malformed metadata.
- [5735611037](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7483#pullrequestreview-5735611037): already addressed. DeepSeek V4/V4.1 dispatch rejects changed or cleared native quantization layouts before the unquantized-export return.
- [5758344253](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7483#pullrequestreview-5758344253): fixed BSHD R2 static-CP recording. uint8 CP shards are gathered and restored in zigzag order before decoding and trimming. Both simulated SP+CP coverage and a real two-process Gloo CP=2 run passed.

Validation on DSW used a separate checkout with the exact changed source hashes. The focused suites passed 188 tests, TQ passed 8, and the tokenizer/fused-forward/MTP suites passed 185, for 381 passing CPU tests and no skips. A direct probe of the real vLLMHttpServer validated mapping/JSON-object handling. All pre-commit hooks passed. The legacy Megatron imports were also moved behind their version guard after this environment exposed the collection failure.

The new GPU reruns use the combined final review fixes from this PR, #7484 and #8032. Their results will be reported separately; the earlier synchronized-runtime measurements are not being presented as validation of those later changes.

The GLM-5.3-Flash checkpoint used by the launcher has root `model_type="glm5_next"`, architecture `Glm5NextForConditionalGeneration`, text type `glm5_next_text`, and vision type `glm5_next_vision`. [Original review](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7483#pullrequestreview-5761250561) · [GLM-5.3-Flash config](https://huggingface.co/zai-org/GLM-5.3-Flash/blob/46a050bec13002ad29ee795c16e61cf4fa3c4a9c/config.json) · [GLM-5.3 text config](https://huggingface.co/zai-org/GLM-5.3/blob/ba32f8c19c8738e2c57c71798c601d477a7bf844/config.json)

The agent loop reads the root config’s `model_type`. With `glm5_next` and its image processor, the resolver selects `VL_DEFAULT` and constructs the processor-backed `VLContinuousTokenBuilder`. Both DSW agent-loop logs (BF16 and FP8) reported `model_type='glm5_next'; falling back to vldefault` and completed rollout. The proposed registry change would target a different model family. [Agent-loop dispatch](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/blob/cd9330c25a9408aeedc87a5abfb88562e9318a05/verl/experimental/agent_loop/agent_loop.py#L537-L542) · [Registry](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/blob/cd9330c25a9408aeedc87a5abfb88562e9318a05/verl/utils/tokenizer/continuous_token_wiring.py#L96-L103) · [Family resolution and construction](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/blob/cd9330c25a9408aeedc87a5abfb88562e9318a05/verl/utils/tokenizer/continuous_token_wiring.py#L206-L279)

Additional CPU-only constructor validation: with `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=''` and the offline Hugging Face cache, the real `SingleTurnAgentLoop` constructed successfully using the actual GLM-5.3-Flash checkpoint and selected `VLContinuousTokenBuilder`. Its multimodal check passed, and a real 112×112 image produced 34 prompt tokens, including 16 image tokens, plus `pixel_values` and `image_grid_thw`. The seven production files on this path match the tested runtime byte for byte. This directly exercises the constructor and prompt-processing path identified by the merge-risk summary; no new family registration is needed. The two-node BF16 and FP8 Geo3K runs also completed step 1 (Pearson 0.9996449351 and 0.9987379909).

- coderabbitai[bot] · comment · 2026-10-12T11:06:17Z · [source](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7483#discussion_r4247306681)

File: `verl/models/mcore/util.py` · line 68

<!-- coderabbit-ai-generated:v1 -->

**🎯 Functional Correctness** | **🟡 Minor** | **⚡ Quick win**

**Check span-scanner termination against the `expand_image_tokens` type layout.**

`expand_image_tokens` emits each span as `0, (1*w, 2)*h, 3`. The scanner moves past `0`. While the next token is `1` (IMAGE), it consumes the run of `1` values and then one extra token (`2`). The outer `position += 1` then consumes `3`. This is correct only if `IMAGE == 1` and the separator and end values are each exactly one token wide. The scanner does not check the end marker. It also does not check that `grid_index` stays within `len(grids)`. A mismatched row therefore splits spans silently or raises an `IndexError` with no clear message. Add an assertion that `row[position-1]` is the end type, and check `grid_index < len(grids)` before each lookup.

🌐 Web query:

```
In NVIDIA’s **`core_v0.19.2`** source, these are `ModelParallelConfig` settings:

- `max_seqlen_per_dp_cp_rank` is an optional maximum sequence length for each DP×CP rank. Megatron uses it to calculate how many subsamples, and their lengths, are assigned to ranks when sequence packing is enabled.
- `sequence_packing_scheduler` accepts `dp_balanced`, described as the DP-balanced scheduler for sequence packing.

That describes the configuration fields; it doesn’t, by itself, spell out the scheduler’s balancing algorithm. ([github.com](https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/blob/core_v0.19.2/megatron/core/model_parallel_config.py))

**Official source:** [NVIDIA/Megatron-LM — `model_parallel_config.py` at `core_v0.19.2`](https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/blob/core_v0.19.2/megatron/core/model_parallel_config.py). The release points to commit `4b4acac`. ([github.com](https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/releases?utm_source=openai))

Citations:

- 1: https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/blob/core_v0.19.2/megatron/core/model_parallel_config.py
- 2: https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/releases?utm_source=openai
```

---



</details>

**Round up the per-rank sequence limit.**

When the configured maximum sequence length is not divisible by `ACTOR_CP`, floor division sets a per-rank limit whose aggregate capacity is too small. Use ceiling division.

     )

**Normalize expanded V4.1 prompt IDs before image expansion.**

The multimodal agent-loop builder can return processor-expanded image-token spans. `AgentLoopWorkerTQ` passes those IDs directly to `expand_image_tokens`, which requires one unexpanded placeholder per `image_grid_hws` row and can raise `ValueError` when the spans are already expanded. Normalize each image span to one placeholder before expansion. Preserve adjacent distinct images as separate spans.

<!-- coderabbit-ai-generated:v1 -->

**🚀 Performance & Scalability** | **🟡 Minor** | **⚡ Quick win**

**Avoid a CUDA synchronization in masked replay.**

When `scores` and `native_rows` are on CUDA, `scores[native_rows]` selects a data-dependent number of rows. Boolean-mask selection can synchronize the device with the host, even when every row is replayed. This adds synchronization to each affected router call. Use fixed-shape top-k with `torch.where`, or a fixed-shape selection method, before relying on this branch as an optimization. ([docs.pytorch.org](https://docs.pytorch.org/docs/stable/generated/torch.nonzero.html?utm_source=openai))

**🗄️ Data Integrity & Integration** | **🟠 Major** | **⚡ Quick win**

**Install the FP8 refit patch for an effective FP8 override.**

If `config.quantization` is unset and `hf_overrides.quantization_config` selects FP8, this guard skips `apply_vllm_quant_patches()`. The supplied patch contract requires that patch before engine construction to record checkpoint layout for weight refit. The engine can therefore start with FP8 while a later weight sync lacks its required layout tracking. Select the patch from the *effective* quantization configuration, not from the absence of an override. vLLM reads `ignored_layers` and `modules_to_not_convert` from that FP8 configuration. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/vllm-project/vllm/v0.29.0/vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/fp8.py))

🌐 Web query:

`Megatron Core dp_balanced max_seqlen_per_dp_cp_rank context parallel sequence packing maximum sequence length floor ceil`

💡 Result:

```
If you’re asking how **`dp_balanced` handles `max_seqlen_per_dp_cp_rank` and rounding**:

- `max_seqlen_per_dp_cp_rank` is a **per-DP×CP-rank sequence-length/memory capacity**. Megatron Core says it helps determine the number and length of subsamples assigned to ranks when a sequence-packing scheduler is enabled; it recommends starting around `max_sequence_length / context_parallel_size`. ([docs.nvidia.com](https://docs.nvidia.com/megatron-core/developer-guide/nightly/apidocs/core/core.model_parallel_config.html))
- `dp_balanced` is documented as a **DP-balanced sequence-packing scheduler**. The docs do **not** specify whether its packing arithmetic uses floor or ceiling, or give an exact formula for splitting sequences. ([docs.nvidia.com](https://docs.nvidia.com/megatron-core/developer-guide/nightly/apidocs/core/core.model_parallel_config.html))
- Don’t conflate this with **dynamic CP**: a separate source describes choosing CP groups based on sequence length, with power-of-two sizing. That does not establish the rounding behavior of `dp_balanced`. (GitHub issue 4586 in NVIDIA-NeMo/Megatron-Bridge (link omitted to avoid creating a cross-reference))

So, from the available documentation, **floor vs. ceil for `dp_balanced` remains unresolved**; it would require checking the implementation for the specific Megatron Core version you’re using.

Citations:

- 1: https://docs.nvidia.com/megatron-core/developer-guide/nightly/apidocs/core/core.model_parallel_config.html
- 2: https://docs.nvidia.com/megatron-core/developer-guide/nightly/apidocs/core/core.model_parallel_config.html
- 3: GitHub issue 4586 in NVIDIA-NeMo/Megatron-Bridge (link omitted to avoid creating a cross-reference)
```

💡 Result:

```
## `dp_balanced` in Megatron Core `core_v0.19.2`

`max_seqlen_per_dp_cp_rank` is the **per-rank token budget** for each packed microbatch. The scheduler uses a total pack limit of:

\[
\text{pack limit} = \texttt{max\_seqlen\_per\_dp\_cp\_rank} \times \texttt{context\_parallel\_size}
\]

So, for example, a per-rank limit of 8,192 with CP=4 allows up to 32,768 tokens in a pack. The configuration requires this value when `sequence_packing_scheduler` is set; `dp_balanced` is the supported scheduler named in the config. ([github.com](https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/blob/main/megatron/core/model_parallel_config.py?utm_source=openai))

At a high level, the path gathers sequence lengths across DP ranks, schedules samples into packs under that limit, then builds packed THD microbatches with sequence-boundary metadata. It can balance packed work across DP ranks, and the number of scheduled microbatches can vary by step. ([docs.nvidia.com](https://docs.nvidia.com/nemo/megatron-bridge/nightly/training/packed-sequences.html?utm_source=openai))

**Version caveat:** The release page confirms `core_v0.19.2` exists, but the implementation details above are established by current Megatron/Bridge documentation, not a directly inspected `data_schedule.py` at that tag. I can’t verify from the sources retrieved here whether the exact packing heuristic or edge-case behavior differs in `core_v0.19.2`. The closest implementation reference is Megatron Core’s `core.datasets.data_schedule` API index. ([github.com](https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/releases?utm_source=openai))

Citations:

- 1: https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/blob/main/megatron/core/model_parallel_config.py?utm_source=openai
- 2: https://docs.nvidia.com/nemo/megatron-bridge/nightly/training/packed-sequences.html?utm_source=openai
- 3: https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/releases?utm_source=openai
```

The available source confirms that Megatron-LM’s **current `main`** supports `sequence_packing_scheduler='dp_balanced'` and requires `max_seqlen_per_dp_cp_rank` when a packing scheduler is set. It describes that value as the maximum sequence length per DP×CP rank. ([github.com](https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/blob/main/megatron/core/model_parallel_config.py?utm_source=openai))

I couldn’t verify those details specifically for **`core_v0.19.2`**: the branch-specific GitHub page was inaccessible, and the search result points to `main`, not that release.

**Use ceiling division for the per-rank sequence limit.**

When `ACTOR_CP=2`, one DP replica is used, and the configured maximum is nondivisible, floor division can classify a valid packed sequence as requiring four ranks. The pinned scheduler cannot place that sequence in the two-rank group and can remain in its leftover loop. Padding does not prevent this because the wrapper passes nonzero `cu_seqlens` lengths to the scheduler.

<details>
<summary>Suggested fix</summary>

```diff
-        ++actor_rollout_ref.actor.megatron.override_transformer_config.max_seqlen_per_dp_cp_rank=$(((MAX_PROMPT_LENGTH + MAX_RESPONSE_LENGTH) / ACTOR_CP))
+        ++actor_rollout_ref.actor.megatron.override_transformer_config.max_seqlen_per_dp_cp_rank=$(((MAX_PROMPT_LENGTH + MAX_RESPONSE_LENGTH + ACTOR_CP - 1) / ACTOR_CP))
```

</details>

**Avoid a full live-name scan for every expert weight.**

For each routed-expert leaf, `resolve_weight_name` evaluates an `any(...)` over `model_weight_names`. This new per-weight call repeats that scan across the LoRA base-sync stream. A model with many expert leaves can spend substantial CPU time reconciling names on every sync. Precompute the relevant expert-prefix membership once per bucket, then reuse it during resolution. **Guard against `_owner` being `None` before calling `do_hijack`.**

If neither `LRUCacheWorkerLoRAManager` nor `_WLM` defines `_load_adapter` in its own `__dict__`, `_owner` stays `None`. This can happen when vLLM moves the method to a base class or renames it. Then `setattr(None, "_load_adapter", ...)` raises `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute '_load_adapter'`. The error message does not identify the cause, and `hijack()` fails.

The `__dict__` check also ignores inherited definitions. Use `hasattr` as a fallback. Raise a clear error if no class has the method.

<details>
<summary>Proposed fix</summary>

```diff
--- "a/verl/utils/vllm/utils.py"
+++ "b/verl/utils/vllm/utils.py"
@@ -130,12 +130,19 @@
             from vllm.lora.worker_manager import WorkerLoRAManager as _WLM
         except ImportError:
             _WLM = None
         _owner = None
         for _c in (LRUCacheWorkerLoRAManager, _WLM):
             if _c is not None and "_load_adapter" in _c.__dict__:
                 _owner = _c
                 break
+        if _owner is None:
+            for _c in (LRUCacheWorkerLoRAManager, _WLM):
+                if _c is not None and hasattr(_c, "_load_adapter"):
+                    _owner = _c
+                    break
+        if _owner is None:
+            raise RuntimeError("Cannot find a vLLM worker LoRA manager class that defines `_load_adapter`.")
         do_hijack(_owner, "_load_adapter", hijack__load_adapter)
 
 
 def is_version_ge(pkg: str = "vllm", minver: str = "0.7.3"):
```

    lines = pathlib.Path(path).read_text(errors='replace').splitlines()

**Align the `min_local_rows` condition between model packing and router replay.**

For DeepSeek V4.1 with context parallel size 1, `model_forward.py` passes `min_local_rows=None`. Router replay passes `csa_window_size` for every `dsv4_hybrid` config. When the packed THD total is below `csa_window_size`, `preprocess_thd_engine` pads the replay route rows to that total. The model tokens stay unpadded, so the route rows and the model rows differ in length. Replay can then fail with a shape error, or attach routes to the wrong tokens. The replay code says that RECORD and REPLAY must pack exactly like the model forward.

Move the `min_local_rows` derivation into one shared helper. Call that helper from `model_forward.py` and from both replay call sites in `router_replay_utils.py`. Confirm which condition is intended for non-V4.1 and CP size 1 configs, and use it in every caller. `model_forward_fused.py` already uses the unconditional form.

**Align BSHD replay with `preprocess_bshd_engine`.**

When `data_format == "bshd"`, `set_router_replay_data` pads routes to the raw `forced_max_seqlen` or longest route length, then scatters them. `preprocess_bshd_engine` rounds that length for TP/CP alignment and may apply additional FP8 alignment. If the raw length is not aligned, replay can provide fewer rows than the model and cause a shape error during `scatter_to_sequence_parallel_region`.

Extract the alignment logic from `preprocess_bshd_engine` into a shared helper and use it for BSHD replay. Alternatively, reject BSHD replay when the required alignment is not 1.

**Do not drop unpaired FP8 weights at the end of a bucket.**

`pending_fp8` is local to one `load_quanted_weights` call. Standard base weights load once per bucket, while the sender can split a weight and its scale across buckets. Each call then drops its incomplete entry without reporting the mismatch. The live BF16 parameter can retain its stale value.

Keep pending entries across the full sync, or fail when a bucket ends with a pending `weight`. At minimum:

<details>
<summary>🛡️ Minimal fail-loud guard</summary>
<dl>
<dd>

```diff
             normalized_weights.append((resolved, tensor))
+        orphaned = [name for name, entry in pending_fp8.items() if "weight" in entry]
+        if orphaned:
+            raise RuntimeError(
+                f"FP8 weights for BF16 live params arrived without their scale in this bucket: {orphaned[:5]}"
+            )
         weights = normalized_weights
```

</dd>
</dl>
</details>

<!-- suggestion_start -->

<details>
<summary>Committable suggestion</summary>
<dl>
<dd>

> ‼️ **IMPORTANT**
> Carefully review the code before committing. Ensure that it accurately replaces the highlighted code, contains no missing lines, and has no issues with indentation. Thoroughly test & benchmark the code to ensure it meets the requirements.

```suggestion
        pending_fp8 = {}
        normalized_weights = []
        for name, tensor in weights:
            resolved = resolve_weight_name(model, name, live_names, routed_expert_prefixes=routed_expert_prefixes)
            leaf = resolved.rsplit(".", 1)[-1]
            if leaf in {"weight", "weight_scale", "weight_scale_inv"}:
                weight_name = resolved.rsplit(".", 1)[0] + ".weight"
                live_dtype = live_weight_dtype(weight_name)
                if live_dtype == torch.bfloat16 and (
                    leaf == "weight" and tensor.dtype in fp8_dtypes or leaf in {"weight_scale", "weight_scale_inv"}
                ):
                    entry = pending_fp8.setdefault(weight_name, {})
                    entry[leaf] = tensor
                    if leaf == "weight":
                        entry["weight_name"] = resolved
                    if "weight" in entry and ("weight_scale" in entry or "weight_scale_inv" in entry):
                        normalized_weights.append(
                            (
                                entry["weight_name"],
                                dequant_fp8_block(
                                    entry["weight"],
                                    entry.get("weight_scale", entry.get("weight_scale_inv")),
                                ),
                            )
                        )
                        del pending_fp8[weight_name]
                    continue
                if (
                    leaf in {"weight_scale", "weight_scale_inv"}
                    and live_dtype is not None
                    and live_dtype not in fp8_dtypes
                ):
                    continue
            normalized_weights.append((resolved, tensor))
        orphaned = [name for name, entry in pending_fp8.items() if "weight" in entry]
        if orphaned:
            raise RuntimeError(
                f"FP8 weights for BF16 live params arrived without their scale in this bucket: {orphaned[:5]}"
            )
        weights = normalized_weights
```

**<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/major-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Major** · **Stability & Availability** · **Quick win**

**Restore the package after the quantized tests.**

When pytest collects this file, `_load_vllm_quant_utils()` replaces `verl.utils.vllm` in `sys.modules` and leaves the stub installed. Later tests can receive that stub instead of the real package. Imports of the real package’s exports then fail according to collection order. Scope all three module substitutions to the tests that need them, and restore their previous values afterward. Python uses `sys.modules` to cache loaded modules. ([docs.python.org](https://docs.python.org/3.12/library/sys.html?utm_source=openai))

**Resolve the nested quantization configuration before treating a null override as BF16.**

When `text_config.quantization_config` is set, vLLM 0.32.0 applies `hf_overrides` to the top-level config, then falls back to `text_config.quantization_config` when the top-level value is `None`. The rollout therefore remains quantized, while this dispatcher returns no quantizer and exports BF16 weights.

<details>
<summary>Suggested fix</summary>
<dl>
<dd>

```diff
         if not has_hf_override:
             native = getattr(hf_config, "quantization_config", None)
             if native is None:
                 native = getattr(getattr(hf_config, "text_config", None), "quantization_config", None)
+        elif native is None:
+            native = getattr(getattr(hf_config, "text_config", None), "quantization_config", None)
         quantization_config = _as_quantization_config(native)
```

Alternatively, clear the nested configuration in the effective vLLM config as well. Update the null-override test to cover a model with `text_config.quantization_config`.

</dd>
</dl>
</details>

**Reject unsupported DeepSeek overrides on this path too.**

When `rollout.quantization` does not set `linear`, `moe`, or `config_file`, a user-supplied `hf_overrides.quantization_config` bypasses the DeepSeek-V4 guard. A `compressed-tensors` override then selects `CompressedTensorsWeightQuantizer`, while native DeepSeek-V4 uses a passthrough `Fp8ConfigWeightQuantizer` for Megatron-Bridge’s mixed FP8/FP4 layout. This can send incompatible weights to vLLM.

Apply the same compatibility check to an effective HF override, or implement conversion for that layout.

**Key `pending_fp8` entries without `.base_layer.`.**

For an unwrappable fused target, the weight can resolve to `compressor.wkv.weight`, while its scale remains `compressor.wkv.base_layer.weight_scale_inv`. These names create separate `pending_fp8` entries. The weight remains unpaired, and the base sync raises `RuntimeError("FP8 weights missing weight_scale or weight_scale_inv ...")`.

Use the normalized name only as the pairing key. Preserve `entry["weight_name"]` as the name passed to the loader.

<details>
<summary>Proposed fix</summary>
<dl>
<dd>

```diff
-                weight_name = resolved.rsplit(".", 1)[0] + ".weight"
-                live_dtype = live_weight_dtype(weight_name)
+                weight_name = resolved.rsplit(".", 1)[0] + ".weight"
+                pending_key = weight_name.replace(".base_layer.", ".", 1)
+                live_dtype = live_weight_dtype(weight_name)
 ...
-                    entry = pending_fp8.setdefault(weight_name, {})
+                    entry = pending_fp8.setdefault(pending_key, {})
 ...
-                        del pending_fp8[weight_name]
+                        del pending_fp8[pending_key]
```

</dd>
</dl>
</details>

**Keep the replay alignment check strict for `deepseek_v41` image prompts.**

For `deepseek_v41` with `image_data`, the comprehension at `vllm_async_server.py:803-809` removes repeated image tokens from both `rollout_prompt_ids` and `actor_prompt_ids`. The equality check at line 811 then compares the collapsed lists, so prompts with different image-token counts can compare as equal and skip the `ValueError` at line 812:
- `[1, 10, 10, 2]` and `[1, 10, 10, 10, 2]` both collapse to `[1, 10, 2]`.
- `[1, 10, 10, 2]` and `[1, 10, 2]` both collapse to `[1, 10, 2]`.

Routing replay needs one route row per rollout token. A silent count mismatch can misalign `routed_experts` with the actor tokens.

`test_generate_checks_expanded_replay_prompt` expects `ValueError` for cases 2, 3, and 5 under both `model_type` values (`tests/workers/rollout/rollout_vllm/test_prompt_alignment_on_cpu.py:31-34` and `78-80`). For `deepseek_v41`, those cases would not raise, so the test would fail when vLLM is installed. Case 5 is `[1, 10, 10, 2, 10, 3]` against `[1, 10, 2, 10, 10, 3]`.

Decide which behavior is intended. Either keep the collapsed comparison and also compare image-token run lengths, or compare the raw sequences. Before choosing raw comparison, confirm that vLLM does not expand image placeholders for `deepseek_v41`. If it does, raw comparison rejects valid requests. Then align the code and the test.

rg -n -F --glob '*.py' -e 'def resolve_weight_name' -e 'resolve_weight_name(' -e 'load_quanted_weights(' -e 'live_weight_dtype(' -e 'match_dtype(' verl tests || test "$?" -eq 1
printf '%s\n' '--- resolver source ---'
resolver=$(rg -l -F --glob '*.py' -- 'def resolve_weight_name' verl | head -n 1)
if [ -n "$resolver" ]; then
  line=$(rg -n -F -- 'def resolve_weight_name' "$resolver" | head -n 1 | cut -d: -f1)
  start=$((line-10)); [ "$start" -lt 1 ] && start=1
  end=$((line+100))
  sed -n "${start},${end}p" "$resolver"
fi
printf '%s\n' '--- focused tests ---'
rg -n -F --glob '*.py' -e 'routed' -e 'expert' -e 'base_layer' -e 'weight_scale' -e 'packed' tests/utils verl/utils/vllm || test "$?" -eq 1
printf '%s\n' '--- DSV4/model references ---'
rg -n -i --glob '*.py' --glob '*.json' --glob '*.yaml' --glob '*.yml' -e 'deepseek.?v4' -e 'deepseek.?v4.1' -e 'glm5' -e 'ds.?v4' -e 'num_experts' -e 'n_routed_experts' tests verl docs examples config 2>/dev/null || test "$?" -eq 1
```

<title>DeepSeek | DeepSeek-V4 Preview: Entering the Era of Affordable Million-Token Context</title>
<location>https://deepseek.com/en/news/v4-preview/</location>
<excerpt>citeturn1view1 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: today; Content type: text/html; Source: open({&quot;ref_id&quot;:&quot;turn0search5&quot;,&quot;lineno&quot;:null}); Total lines: 101
L0: 【0†DeepSeek Web†chat.deepseek.com】【1†DeepSeek Harness】【2†API Platform†platform.deepseek.com】【3†API Docs†api-docs.deepseek.com】【4†Downloads】【5†Join Us†talent.deepseek.com】
L1: 
L2: [Button: 中文][Button: EN]
L3: 
L4: News April 24, 2026
L5: # DeepSeek-V4 Preview: Entering the Era of Affordable Million-Token Context
L6: 
L7: 【6†Read Paper†arxiv.org】
L8: 
L9: DeepSeek-V4 Preview is officially live &amp; open-sourced! Welcome to the era of cost-effective 1M context length.
L10: 
L11:   * DeepSeek-V4-Pro: 1.6T total / 49B active params. Performance rivaling the world&#39;s top closed-source models.
L12:   * DeepSeek-V4-Flash: 284B total / 13B active params. Your fast, efficient, and economical choice.
L13: Try it now at 【0†chat.deepseek.com†chat.deepseek.com】 via Expert Mode / Instant Mode. API is updated &amp; available today!
L14: 
L15:   * 【7†DeepSeek-V4 Open Weights - Hugging Face†huggingface.co】
L16:   * 【6†DeepSeek-V4 Technical Report - arXiv†arxiv.org】
L17: 
L18: 【8†Image: DeepSeek V4 Specifications】
L19: 
L20: * * *
L21: ## DeepSeek-V4-Pro
L22: 
L23:   * Enhanced Agentic Capabilities: Open-source SOTA in Agentic Coding benchmarks.
L24:   * Rich World Knowledge: Leads all current open models, trailing only Gemini-3.1-Pro.
L25:   * World-Class Reasoning: Beats all current open models in Math/STEM/Coding, rivaling top closed-source models.
L26: 
L27: 【9†Image: DeepSeek V4 Pro Benchmark】
L28: 
L29: * * *
L30: ## DeepSeek-V4-Flash
L31: 
L32:   * Reasoning capabilities closely approach V4-Pro.
L33:   * Performs on par with V4-Pro on simple Agent tasks.
L34:   * Smaller parameter size, faster response times, and highly cost-effective API pricing.
L35: 
L36: 【10†Image: DeepSeek V4 Flash Benchmark】
L37: 
L38: * * *
L39: ## Structural Innovation &amp; Ultra-High Context Efficiency
L40: 
L41:   * Novel Attention: Token-wise compression + DSA (DeepSeek Sparse Attention).
L42:   * Peak Efficiency: World-leading long context with drastically reduced compute &amp; memory costs.
L43:   * 1M Standard: 1M context is now the default across all official DeepSeek services.
L44: 
L45: 【11†Image: DeepSeek V4 Efficiency】
L46: 
L47: * * *
L48: ## Dedicated Optimizations for Agent Capabilities
L49: 
L50:   * DeepSeek-V4 is seamlessly integrated with leading AI agents like Claude Code, OpenClaw &amp; OpenCode.
L51:   * Already driving our in-house agentic coding at DeepSeek.
L52: 
L53: The figure below showcases a sample PDF generated by DeepSeek-V4-Pro.
L54: 
L55: 【12†Image: V4 Pro PPT Sample】
L56: 
L57: * * *
L58: ## API is Available Today!
L59: 
L60:   * Keep base_url, just update model to `deepseek-v4-pro` or `deepseek-v4-flash`.
L61:   * Supports OpenAI ChatCompletions &amp; Anthropic APIs.
L62:   * Both models support 1M context &amp; dual modes (Thinking / Non-Thinking): 【13†Thinking Mode Guide - API Docs†api-docs.deepseek.com】
L63: 
L64: &gt; Note: deepseek-chat &amp; deepseek-reasoner will be fully retired and inaccessible after Jul 24th, 2026, 15:59 (UTC Time). Currently routing to deepseek-v4-flash non-thinking/thinking.
L65: 
L66: 【14†Image: V4 API Pricing】
L67: * * *
L68: 
L69:   * Amid recent attention, a quick reminder: please rely only on our official accounts for DeepSeek news. Statements from other channels do not reflect our views.
L70:   * Thank you for your continued trust. We remain committed to longtermism, advancing steadily toward our ultimate goal of AGI.
L71: 
L72:   * 【15†DeepSeek-V4-Pro】
L73:   * 【16†DeepSeek-V4-Flash】
L74:   * 【17†Structural Innovation &amp; Ultra-High Context Efficiency】
L75:   * 【18†Dedicated Optimizations for Agent Capabilities】
L76:   * 【19†API is Available Today!】
L77: 【20†浙ICP备2023025841号†beian.miit.gov.cn】【21†浙B2-20250178†tsm.miit.gov.cn】【22†浙公网安备33010502011812号†beian.mps.gov.cn】
L78: 
L79: service@deepseek.com
L80: 
L81: 【23†Image: QR Code】Scan to follow DeepSeek on WeChat
L82:

L84: 
L85: 【4†Downloads】【0†DeepSeek Web†chat.deepseek.com】【1†DeepSeek Harness】【2†API Platform†platform.deepseek.com】【3†API Docs†api-docs.deepseek.com】【24†API Pricing†api-docs.deepseek.com】【25†Service Status†status.deepseek.com】
L86:

<title>DeepSeek | DeepSeek-V4 Preview: Entering the Era of Affordable Million-Token Context</title>
<location>https://deepseek.com/en/news/v4-preview/</location>
<excerpt>citeturn0search5 [wordlim: 200] Published: 6 months ago; Crawled: yesterday;   * Smaller parameter size, faster response times, and highly cost-effective API pricing. ...   * 1M Standard: 1M context is now the default across all official DeepSeek services. ...   * Keep base_url, just update model to `deepseek-v4-pro` or `deepseek-v4-flash`.
</excerpt>
</source>
<source kind="search">
<title>DeepSeek-V4 · Hugging Face</title>
<location>https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/model_doc/deepseek_v4</location>
<excerpt>citeturn0search0 [wordlim: 200] Published: 5 months ago; Crawled: today; V4-Pro default: 2× HCA bootstrap + interleaved CSA / HCA. ... Only relevant if `config.is_decoder=True` or when the model is a decoder-only generative model. ... V4 narrows the global `ALLOWED_LAYER_TYPES` to the three attention-block types and two MLP-block types it actually ships with, on top of the standard length / type-membership checks.
Parameters

  * vocab_size (`int`, optional, defaults to `129280`) — Vocabulary size of the model. Defines the number of different tokens that can be represented by the `input_ids`.
  * hidden_size (`int`, optional, defaults to `4096`) — Dimension of the hidden representations.
  * moe_intermediate_size (`int`, optional, defaults to `2048`) — Intermediate size of the routed expert MLPs.
  * num_hidden_layers (`int`, optional, defaults to `43`) — Number of hidden layers in the Transformer decoder.
  * num_attention_heads (`int`, optional, defaults to `64`) — Number of attention heads for each attention layer in the Transformer decoder.
  * num_key_value_heads (`int`, optional, defaults to `1`) — This is the number of key_value heads that should be used to implement Grouped Query Attention. If `num_key_value_heads=num_attention_heads`, the model will use Multi Head Attention (MHA), if `num_key_value_heads=1` the model will use Multi Query Attention (MQA) otherwise GQA is used. When converting a multi-head checkpoint to a GQA checkpoint, each group key and value head should be constructed by meanpooling all the original heads within that group. For more details, check out this paper. If it is not specified, will default to `num_attention_heads`.
  * head_dim (`int`, optional, defaults to `512`) — The attention head dimension. If None, it will default to hidden_size // num_attention_heads
  * q_lora_rank (`int`, optional, defaults to `1024`) — Rank of the LoRA matrices for query projections.
  * num_experts_per_tok (`int`, optional, defaults to `6`) — Number of experts to route each token to. This is the top-k value for the token-choice routing.
  * n_routed_experts (`int`, optional, defaults to `256`) — Number of routed experts.
  * n_shared_experts (`int`, optional, defaults to `1`) — Number of shared experts.
  * scoring_func (`str`, optional, defaults to `sqrtsoftplus`) — Router activation — `sqrtsoftplus`, `softmax`, or `sigmoid`.
  * norm_topk_prob (`bool`, optional, defaults to `True`) — Whether to normalize the weights of the routed experts.
  * routed_scaling_factor (`float`, optional, defaults to `1.5`) — Scaling factor or routed experts.
</excerpt>
</source>

L3592: title: &quot;vLLM Ascend A5 image required&quot;
L3593: body: &quot;Mixed serving on one 8-card 950DT node uses 4 of 8 NPUs (DP4+EP). FP8 (Preview) runs MTP k=1; FP8 (0731) runs DSpark k=5, verified on 950PR and offered here on the same A5 command. Pin quay.io/ascend/vllm-ascend:v0.23.0-a5 — the NVIDIA pip wheel cannot run here. Set HCCL_BUFFSIZE=1024 before launch. DeepSeek-V4 on 950 also needs the indexer quant_mode fix in vllm-project/vllm-ascend#16104 — without it the image the tag currently ships aborts during warmup with QuantLightningIndexerV2 EZ0021.&quot;
L3603: body: &quot;Mixed serving on one 8-card 950PR node uses 4 of 8 NPUs (DP4+EP). FP8 (Preview) runs MTP k=1, same command as 950DT; FP8 (0731) runs DSpark k=5.
