````
L3622: ## Overview
L3623: 
L3624: DeepSeek-V4-Flash is a 284B-total / 13B-active MoE model in the V4 preview family.
L3625: 
L3626: It pairs a **hybrid attention stack** — Compressed Sparse Attention (CSA) + Heavily
L3627: 
L3628: Compressed Attention (HCA) — with **Manifold-Constrained Hyper-Connections (mHC)** to
L3629: 
L3630: reach 27% of V3.2&#39;s per-token inference FLOPs and 10% of V3.2&#39;s KV cache at 1M
L3631: 
L3632: context. Pre-trained on 32T+ tokens; post-training is a two-stage pipeline (domain-
L3633: specific expert cultivation + unified consolidation via on-policy distillation).
L3634: 
L3635: Checkpoint is **FP4+FP8 mixed**: MoE expert weights are stored in FP4 while the
L3636: 
L3637: remaining (attention / norm / router) params stay in FP8.
L3638: 
L3639: An **NVFP4 variant** (`nvidia/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-NVFP4`) is also available — NVIDIA
L3640: 
L3641: modelopt re-quantizes the MoE experts to standard NVFP4 while attention, shared
L3642: 
L3643: experts, router head, and MTP stay FP8. Pick it from the **Variant** row; it runs on
L3649: ## Checkpoints
L3650: 
L3651: Four checkpoints are on the **Variant** row. They differ in *which weights you serve*
L3652: 
L3653: and *which draft module ships with them* — the speculative method itself is picked on
L3654: 
L3655: the **Spec Decoding** row.
L3656: 
L3657: | Variant | Repo | Draft | Notes |
L3658: 
L3659: | :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
L3660: 
L3661: | **FP8 (0731)** (default) | `deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731` | DSpark | Official release, new weights + DSpark |
L3662: 
L3663: | **FP8 (Preview)** | `deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Flash` | MTP | Preview FP4+FP8 mixed weights |
L3664: | **NVFP4** | `nvidia/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-NVFP4` | MTP | modelopt re-quant, Blackwell |
L3665: 
L3666: | **DSpark** | `deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-DSpark` | DSpark | Preview weights + fused DSpark module |
L3667: 
L3668: **0731 is the official DeepSeek-V4-Flash release** and the default here, superseding
L3669: 
L3670: the preview with substantially stronger agentic capability — 82.7 on Terminal Bench
L3671: 
L3672: 2.1 and 54.4 on DeepSWE, versus 61.8 and 7.3 for the preview. It beats
L3673: DeepSeek-V4-Pro (Preview) on every agentic benchmark DeepSeek published despite its
L3674: 
L3675: far smaller activated parameter count. The preview weights remain available as the
L3676: 
L3677: **FP8 (Preview)** variant for reproducing earlier results.
L3678: 
L3679: **DSpark** is *not* new weights — it is the preview checkpoint with a speculative
L3680: 
L3681: decoding module attached (see [DeepSpec](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepSpec)).
L3682: 
L3683: Both fused checkpoints add a `dspark_*` block to `config.json`; the preview and NVFP4
L3721: DeepSeek recommends `top_p = 0.95` for agentic scenarios (`1.0` otherwise) with
L3722: 
L3723: `temperature = 1.0`. Allow up to **384K output tokens** at the `high` and `max`
L3724: 
L3725: levels.
L3726: 
L3727: Note that neither fused checkpoint ships a Jinja chat template — the repos provide an
L3728: 
L3729: `encoding/` folder with `encode_messages` / `parse_message_from_completion_text`
L3730: 
L3731: helpers instead. Serving through vLLM with `--tokenizer-mode deepseek_v4` (the
L3732: 
L3733: **Tool Calling** pill) applies the built-in DeepSeek-V4 encoding, so the OpenAI-
L3735: ### OpenAI Client Example
L3736: 
L3737: For DeepSeek-V4, keep reasoning controls in `chat_template_kwargs`, as it exposes a
L3738: 
L3739: custom **Think Max** mode via `&quot;reasoning_effort&quot;: &quot;max&quot;`.
L3740: 
L3741: ```python
L3742: 
L3743: from openai import OpenAI
L3744: 
L3745: client = OpenAI(base_url=&quot;http://localhost:8000/v1&quot;, api_key=&quot;EMPTY&quot;)
L3746:

L3748:

L3750: 
L3751: # Non-think

L3753: resp = client.chat.completions.create(
L3754:

L3756: 
L3757: messages=messages,
L3758: 
L3759: )
L3816: ### H100 (8×80 GB)
L3817: 
L3818: The FP8 (0731) and DSpark checkpoints are selectable on H100. A reported
L3819: 
L3820: 8×H100 TP8 + EP deployment of 0731 served requests with vLLM 0.26.0, while
L3821:

L3823: 
L3824: DSpark. See [vLLM #52065](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/52065).
L3825:

L3827:
````

**Precompute the live-name suffix lookup.**

`live_weight_dtype` calls `match_dtype` for each `weight`, `weight_scale`, and `weight_scale_inv` leaf. Each call can perform up to four full scans: two name candidates and up to two mapped candidates. Routed expert leaves can miss because the live model exposes fused expert parameters, not per-expert parameter names.

A DSV4-sized model has 43 layers and 256 routed experts. The exact Python time depends on the concrete vLLM parameter namespace and update contents, so a seconds-scale or billion-comparison figure is not established here. However, the repeated scans are not negligible at this model scale and are unnecessary.

Build a suffix-to-dtype lookup once before the weight loop. This preserves the current exact-or-dotted-suffix matching and unique-dtype behavior.

<details>
<summary>Suggested fix</summary>
<dl>
<dd>

```diff
--- "a/verl/utils/vllm/vllm_quant_utils.py"
+++ "b/verl/utils/vllm/vllm_quant_utils.py"
@@ -276,14 +276,15 @@
                 prefix, leaf = name.rsplit(".", 1)
                 candidates.append(f"{prefix}.base_layer.{leaf}")
 
+        suffix_dtypes: dict[str, set] = {}
+        for live_name, parameter in live_parameters.items():
+            segments = live_name.split(".")
+            for i in range(len(segments)):
+                suffix_dtypes.setdefault(".".join(segments[i:]), set()).add(parameter.dtype)
+
             def match_dtype(candidate):
-                matches = [
-                    parameter
-                    for live_name, parameter in live_parameters.items()
-                    if live_name == candidate or live_name.endswith(f".{candidate}")
-                ]
-                dtypes = {parameter.dtype for parameter in matches}
+                dtypes = suffix_dtypes.get(candidate, ())
                 return next(iter(dtypes)) if len(dtypes) == 1 else None
 
             def mapped_candidates(candidate):
                 yield candidate
```

</dd>
</dl>
</details>

`@HollowMan6` Not yet. I checked commit `6213fdaf`. In `verl/utils/vllm/vllm_quant_utils.py`, Line 279–286, `match_dtype()` still scans all `live_parameters` for each candidate.

The fix is to build the suffix-to-dtype lookup once, outside `live_weight_dtype()`, and use that lookup in `match_dtype()`. Preserve exact and dotted-suffix matching, and return a dtype only when the match has one distinct dtype.

**Fix the undefined `CUDA_ALLOC_CONF` variable. The launcher aborts at this line.**

The script runs with `set -u` (Line 2). The script never defines `CUDA_ALLOC_CONF`. Line 12 exports `PYTORCH_CUDA_ALLOC_CONF`, not `CUDA_ALLOC_CONF`.

Bash expands the `run` arguments before `run` is called. The script therefore fails with `CUDA_ALLOC_CONF: unbound variable` before it starts training. `DRY_RUN=1` hits the same failure. The script works only if the caller has already exported `CUDA_ALLOC_CONF`.

Use the variable that Line 12 exports. Quote the whole override so `:` and `=` stay in one argument.

**Save checkpoints in the default training run.**

`SAVE_FREQ=-1` disables every checkpoint save, including the final save. A default 400-step run therefore leaves no trained adapter in `CKPTS_DIR`. Set a positive default save frequency; callers can still override `SAVE_FREQ`. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/verl-project/verl/b403062a489429893ef19f717dd0ab100c65d09d/verl/trainer/ppo/v1/trainer_base.py))

  

<details>
<summary>Proposed fix</summary>
<dl>
<dd>

```diff
--- "a/examples/tuning/lora/run_deepseek_v41_flash_megatron.sh"
+++ "b/examples/tuning/lora/run_deepseek_v41_flash_megatron.sh"
@@ -160,7 +160,7 @@
 VLLM_LINEAR_BACKEND=${VLLM_LINEAR_BACKEND:-auto}
 TOTAL_TRAINING_STEPS=${TOTAL_TRAINING_STEPS:-400}
 TOTAL_EPOCHS=${TOTAL_EPOCHS:-13}
-SAVE_FREQ=${SAVE_FREQ:--1}
+SAVE_FREQ=${SAVE_FREQ:-50}
 PARAM_OFFLOAD=${PARAM_OFFLOAD:-False}
 OPTIMIZER_OFFLOAD=${OPTIMIZER_OFFLOAD:-False}
 OPTIMIZER_OFFLOAD_FRACTION=${OPTIMIZER_OFFLOAD_FRACTION:-1.0}
```

**Read nested `text_config` quantization overrides.**

If `hf_overrides.text_config.quantization_config` is `null`, this helper reports no override. The dispatcher then selects the checkpoint’s native text-level FP8 config. vLLM 0.32.0 applies that nested override and selects an unquantized rollout instead. The trainer can therefore export FP8 weights for a rollout that expects unquantized weights. Resolve nested overrides with the same precedence that vLLM uses, including an explicit `null`. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/vllm-project/vllm/v0.32.0/vllm/config/model.py))

**Reject FP8 overrides that change the DeepSeek-V4 weight layout.**

If `hf_overrides.quantization_config` selects FP8 without the checkpoint’s `weight_block_size`, this guard accepts the override. **Apply the same ignore-list mapping on the trainer side.**

For models whose vLLM mapper does not rewrite `model.layers.` names, such as `glm4_1v`, the trainer alias can disagree with vLLM. Consider an FP8 override that excludes `model.layers.0.mlp.down_proj`. The trainer matches the exported `model.language_model.layers.0.mlp.down_proj` through the `language_model.` alias and does not quantize it. vLLM keeps the override name unchanged, names the layer `language_model.model.layers.0.mlp.down_proj`, and does not ignore it. vLLM then allocates the layer as FP8. Weight sync does not send the `weight_scale_inv` tensor for that layer, so the rollout can load a BF16 tensor into an FP8 parameter.

Make the trainer compare against the names vLLM uses. Apply the model's `hf_to_vllm_mapper` to the ignore list before the comparison. Keep the `language_model.` alias only for exclusions that the mapper rewrites to the same module name.

Rechecked against `77068437c7942b47e4e7dabda4a4eea6a9d8c646`. This mismatch has been addressed.

The trainer no longer uses an unconditional `language_model.` alias. `get_fp8_ignored_layers` expands exact VLM text exclusions in both the trainer exporter and the rollout configuration. The rollout supplies those names before vLLM applies the model's native `hf_to_vllm_mapper` once. Original exclusions are retained, and substring/suffix matching is unchanged. This covers both mappers that rewrite `model.layers.*` and GLM/Qwen3-VL mappers that leave that spelling unchanged.

Validation of this implementation: 54 focused CPU tests passed in `test_mxfp8_ignored_layers_on_cpu.py` and `test_rollout_quantization_overrides_on_cpu.py`. On the DSW installation, 21 additional CPU probes used real vLLM model mappers and `Fp8Config.get_quant_method` across GLM4.1V, GLM5Next, Qwen2-VL, Qwen2.5-VL and Qwen3-VL, including nested configs/overrides and reverse aliases. The excluded layer and the 64x4096 projection remained unquantized, while the adjacent layer selected FP8. These are CPU/configuration checks; the new GLM BF16/FP8 and DeepSeek V4.1 step-1 GPU validation is still in progress.
